\mathtt{pref}_{s}({{r}_{\min}})|_{\mathtt{write}}=\mathtt{pref}_{L(e_{\bar{i}})}({{r}_{\min}})|_{\mathtt{write}}
.
Indeed, we have:

\mathtt{pref}_{s}({{r}_{\min}})|_{\mathtt{write}}=\mathtt{pref}_{L(e_{i})}({{w}_{\min}})|_{\mathtt{write}}=\mathtt{pref}_{s_{i}}({{w}_{\min}})|_{\mathtt{write}}=\mathtt{pref}_{s_{\bar{i}}}({{w}_{\min}})|_{\mathtt{write}}

where the equalities are justified by:
The construction of
s
,
the fact due to
Section

G.2
and the definition of
s_{i}
that
{w}_{\min}\in L(e_{i})\preceq_{\mathsf{P}}s_{i}
,
and the fact due to
Observation

70
that

s_{i}|_{\mathtt{write}}=s_{\bar{i}}|_{\mathtt{write}}
.

Similarly, since
{r}_{\min}\in L(e_{\bar{i}})\preceq_{\mathsf{P}}s_{\bar{i}}
we have

\mathtt{pref}_{L(e_{\bar{i}})}({{r}_{\min}})|_{\mathtt{write}}=\mathtt{pref}_{s_{\bar{i}}}({{r}_{\min}})|_{\mathtt{write}}
.

Overall it remains to show that

\mathtt{pref}_{s_{\bar{i}}}({{w}_{\min}})|_{\mathtt{write}}=\mathtt{pref}_{s_{\bar{i}}}({{r}_{\min}})|_{\mathtt{write}}
.
Equivalently, we need to show that for a write operation
w
, we have
w<_{s_{\bar{i}}}{w}_{\min}\iff w<_{s_{\bar{i}}}{r}_{\min}
.
Indeed,
from
Section

G.2
we have
{r}_{\min}<_{s_{\bar{i}}}{w}_{\min}
which gives the implication
w<_{s_{\bar{i}}}{r}_{\min}\implies w<_{s_{\bar{i}}}{w}_{\min}
.
If we assume for contradiction the other implication does not hold, then there must exist a write operation
w
such that
{r}_{\min}<_{s_{\bar{i}}}w<_{s_{\bar{i}}}{w}_{\min}
, but this is impossible due to
Section

G.2
.

2.

\mathsf{h}({e_{i}})\sqsubseteq s
:

As
\mathsf{h}({e_{i}})\sqsubseteq L(e_{i})
and by construction
\mathsf{set}(s)=\mathsf{set}(L(e_{i}))
,
using
Lemma

33
it is sufficient to show that
o^{\prime}<_{\mathsf{h}({e_{i}})}o
implies
o^{\prime}<_{s}o
for all
o,o^{\prime}\in\mathsf{set}(s)
.

Indeed, from
o^{\prime}<_{\mathsf{h}({e_{i}})}o
and the fact that
o,o^{\prime}\in\mathsf{set}(s)=\mathsf{set}(L(e_{i}))

we get by definition of linearizability that
o^{\prime}<_{L(e_{i})}o
.
Consider the following cases:

•

If
o,o^{\prime}\in\mathsf{set}(L(e_{i}))\setminus\{{{r}_{\min}}\}
then by construction
o^{\prime}<_{s}o
.

•

If
o^{\prime}={r}_{\min}
then using
Section

G.2
we have
{w}_{\min}<_{L(e_{i})}{r}_{\min}=o^{\prime}<_{L(e_{i})}o
and so
{w}_{\min}<_{L(e_{i})}o
,
This means
o\notin\mathtt{pref}_{L(e_{i})}({{w}_{\min}})=S
.

o={r}_{\min}
is impossible as
o^{\prime}<_{L(e_{i})}o
implies that
o\neq o^{\prime}={r}_{\min}
,
and thus overall
o\in\bar{S}
,
which implies
o^{\prime}={r}_{\min}<_{s}o
.

•

If
o={r}_{\min}
, due to
Section

G.2
it is not possible that
{w}_{\min}\leq_{L(e_{i})}o^{\prime}<_{\mathsf{h}({e_{i}})}{r}_{\min}=o
,
and so we must have that
o^{\prime}<_{L(e_{i})}{w}_{\min}
and thus

o^{\prime}\in\mathtt{pref}_{L(e_{i})}({{w}_{\min}})=S

This implies
o^{\prime}<_{s}{r}_{\min}=o
.

3.

s\precsim^{\mathsf{w*}}L(e_{i})
:

By construction
\mathsf{set}(s)=\mathsf{set}(L(e_{i}))
.
It remains to show that
([\mathtt{write}]\mathbin{;}<_{s})\subseteq<_{L(e_{i})}
.

Let
o,o^{\prime}
be operations such that
{\mathtt{method}}(o)=\mathtt{write}
and
o<_{s}o^{\prime}
.
In particular
o\neq{r}_{\min}
.
Consider the following options:

•

If
o^{\prime}\neq{r}_{\min}
, then
o,o^{\prime}\in\mathsf{set}(L(e_{i}))\setminus\{{{r}_{\min}}\}
and we must have by construction that
o<_{L(e_{i})}o^{\prime}
.

•

If
o^{\prime}={r}_{\min}
, then
o<_{s}o^{\prime}={r}_{\min}
implies that
o\in S
and thus
o<_{L(e_{i})}{w}_{\min}
.
Due to
Section

G.2
we have
{w}_{\min}<_{L(e_{i})}{r}_{\min}
and thus
o<_{L(e_{i})}{r}_{\min}=o^{\prime}
, as required.

4.

s|_{\mathtt{write}}=L(e_{i})|_{\mathtt{write}}
:

Given any pair of write operations
w,w^{\prime}\in\mathsf{set}(s)
we have that
w,w^{\prime}\in\mathsf{set}(L(e_{i}))\setminus\{{{r}_{\min}}\}
and so by construction
w<_{s}w^{\prime}\iff w<_{L(e_{i})}w^{\prime}
.

From
Lemma

67
we deduce that
s\sim^{\mathsf{w*}}L(e_{i})

and in particular
([\mathtt{write}]\mathbin{;}<_{L(e_{i})})\subseteq<_{s}
.
Due to
Section

G.2
we have
{w}_{\min}<_{L(e_{i})}{r}_{\min}
,
and so using the inclusion we get
{w}_{\min}<_{s}{r}_{\min}
.
By construction of
s
this means
{w}_{\min}\in S=\mathsf{set}(\mathtt{pref}_{L(e_{i})}({{w}_{\min}}))
, a contradiction.

We conclude that
R=\emptyset
.∎

We now extend the above result,
and show the ordering between
any two operations
is maintained, as long as there is some write operation between them.
(The previous lemma is then the specific case where one of the operations is itself a write.)
Formally we prove the following:

Lemma 72
.

If
I\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{ws}}
,

L\in\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}^{\precsim_{\mathsf{P}}^{\mathsf{ww}}}(I)
is
\precsim^{\mathsf{w*}}
-minimal,
and
w
is a write operation,
then

(\leq_{s_{1}}\mathbin{;}[w]\mathbin{;}\leq_{s_{1}})=(\leq_{s_{2}}\mathbin{;}[w]\mathbin{;}\leq_{s_{2}})\cap(\mathsf{set}(s_{1})\times\mathsf{set}(s_{1}))

for all
e_{1},e_{2}\in\mathsf{E}({I})
such that
e_{1}\preceq_{\mathsf{P}}e_{2}
,
where
s_{1}=L(e_{1})\oplus(L(e_{2})|_{\mathtt{write}})
and
s_{2}=L(e_{2})
.

That is, every two operations present in
s_{1}
(and thus in
s_{2}
) are ordered the same way by
s_{1}
,
s_{2}
,
as long as there is some write operation
w
between them.
(Which leaves only the ordering between reads with no write between them, which might not be maintained.)

Proof.

As
(\leq_{s_{1}}\mathbin{;}[w]\mathbin{;}\leq_{s_{1}})=(\leq_{s_{1}}\mathbin{;}[w]\mathbin{;}\leq_{s_{1}})\cap(\mathsf{set}(s_{1})\times\mathsf{set}(s_{1}))
it is sufficient to show that

(\leq_{s_{i}}\mathbin{;}[w]\mathbin{;}\leq_{s_{i}})\cap(\mathsf{set}(s_{1})\times\mathsf{set}(s_{1}))\subseteq(\leq_{s_{\bar{i}}}\mathbin{;}[w]\mathbin{;}\leq_{s_{\bar{i}}})

for
(i,\bar{i})\in\{{(1,2),(2,1)}\}
.

Let
{\langle{o,o^{\prime}}\rangle}\in(\leq_{s_{i}}\mathbin{;}[w]\mathbin{;}\leq_{s_{i}})\cap(\mathsf{set}(s_{1})\times\mathsf{set}(s_{1}))
.
Then
o\leq_{s_{i}}w\leq_{s_{i}}o^{\prime}
and in particular
o,w,o^{\prime}\in\mathsf{set}(s_{i})
.
We can show
o,w,o^{\prime}\in\mathsf{set}(s_{\bar{i}})
.
Indeed, Consider both options for
i
:

•

If
i=1
then
o,w,o^{\prime}\in\mathsf{set}(s_{2})
follows from
o,w,o^{\prime}\in\mathsf{set}(s_{1})
and
\mathsf{set}(s_{1})\subseteq\mathsf{set}(s_{2})
, which follows from
Lemma

69
.

•

If
i=2
then
o,o^{\prime}\in\mathsf{set}(s_{1})
follows from
{\langle{o,o^{\prime}}\rangle}\in(\mathsf{set}(s_{1})\times\mathsf{set}(s_{1}))
and
w\in\mathsf{set}(s_{1})
follows from
w\in\mathsf{set}(s_{2})
and
s_{1}|_{\mathtt{write}}=s_{2}|_{\mathtt{write}}
, which follows from
Observation

70
.

We deduce that
o,w,o^{\prime}
are ordered by
\leq_{s_{\bar{i}}}
.
Moreover, we have:

•

o\leq_{s_{\bar{i}}}w
:

If
o=w
this is trivial.
Otherwise,
o<_{s_{i}}w
.
If we assume for contradiction
w<_{s_{\bar{i}}}o
we get
{\langle{w,o}\rangle}\in([\mathtt{write}]\mathbin{;}(<_{s_{\bar{i}}}\cap<_{s_{i}}^{-1}))
,
contradicting
Lemma

71
.
We deduce
o<_{s_{\bar{i}}}w
.

•

w\leq_{s_{\bar{i}}}o^{\prime}
:

If
o^{\prime}=w
this is trivial.
Otherwise,
w<_{s_{i}}o^{\prime}
.
If we assume for contradiction
o^{\prime}<_{s_{\bar{i}}}w
we get
{\langle{w,o^{\prime}}\rangle}\in([\mathtt{write}]\mathbin{;}(<_{s_{i}}\cap<_{s_{\bar{i}}}^{-1}))
,
contradicting
Lemma

71
.
We deduce
w<_{s_{\bar{i}}}o^{\prime}
.

Overall,
o\leq_{s_{\bar{i}}}w\leq_{s_{\bar{i}}}o^{\prime}
and thus
{\langle{o,o^{\prime}}\rangle}\in(\leq_{s_{\bar{i}}}\mathbin{;}[w]\mathbin{;}\leq_{s_{\bar{i}}})

as required.
∎

Finally, we use the above to show that while a
\precsim^{\mathsf{w*}}
-minimal write strong linearization does not have to be decisive,
one can transform it into a decisive linearization which is also minimal, thus not losing any of the other properties established so far.
Specifically, we modify an arbitrary minimal mapping to ensure that the ordering between reads that have no write between them does not change once they are linearized.
As
Lemma

72
ensures the ordering between operations is maintained in all other cases,
we overall obtain decisiveness.

Lemma 73
.

If
I\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{ws}}
, then there exists a write strong and decisive linearization that is
\precsim^{\mathsf{w*}}
-minimal in
\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}^{\precsim_{\mathsf{P}}^{\mathsf{ww}}}(I)
.

Proof.

Let
L\in\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}^{\precsim_{\mathsf{P}}^{\mathsf{ww}}}(I)
be a
\precsim^{\mathsf{w*}}
-minimal write strong linearization as guaranteed to exist by
Lemma

66
.
Let
e\in\mathsf{E}({I})
.

First we define the following relation:

R(e)=(\leq_{L(e)}\mathbin{;}[\mathtt{write}]\mathbin{;}\leq_{L(e)})\setminus[\mathsf{set}(L(e))]

As we later prove, this is a strict partial order.

R(e)
orders operations the same way
<_{L(e)}
does,
as long as the operations have some write operation between them.
Otherwise, the ordering between the operations is discarded.
The idea is to only keep the ordering that will be preserved when extending an execution due to
Lemma

72
,
and to reorder the reads between any two writes in a way which overall guarantees decisiveness.

Specifically, we will use the following ordering for reads with no write between them:

\mathtt{rfr}(e)=\{{{\langle{r,r^{\prime}}\rangle}\in\mathsf{set}(L(e)|_{\mathtt{read}})^{2}\;|\;r\text{ responds before }r^{\prime}\text{ in }e}\}\setminus(R(e)\cup R(e)^{-1})

As we show later, this is also a strict partial order.
This relation orders reads that have no write between them in
L(e)
, based on which one responded first.

We use the above relations to construct a linearization mapping
L^{\prime}\in\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}^{\precsim_{\mathsf{P}}^{\mathsf{ww}}}(I)
,
where
L^{\prime}(e)
is defined for every
e\in\mathsf{E}({I})
as the sequence over
\mathsf{set}(L(e))
induced by the relation
R(e)\cup\mathtt{rfr}(e)
,
that, as we prove later, is a strict total order.
The idea is that operations with a write between then in
L(e)
are ordered like in
L(e)
,
and operations with no write between them in
L(e)
are two reads, ordered based on which one responded first.

Finally, we prove that
L^{\prime}
has all desired properties.
We proceed now with filling out the details of the proof.
First we show that
R(e)
and
\mathtt{rfr}(e)
are indeed strict partial orders:

Claim 73.1:

R(e)
is a strict partial order.

{claimproof}

Clearly,
R(e)\subseteq<_{L(e)}
.
Irreflexivity
then immediately follows from the inclusion in a strict partial order.
For transitivity, assume
{\langle{o_{1},o_{2}}\rangle},{\langle{o_{2},o_{3}}\rangle}\in R(e)
.
From
{\langle{o_{1},o_{2}}\rangle}\in R(e)
we have that there exists a a write operation
w
such that
o_{1}\leq_{L(e)}w\leq_{L(e)}o_{2}
.
From
{\langle{o_{2},o_{3}}\rangle}\in R(e)
we get that
o_{2}\leq_{L(e)}o_{3}
,
and so overall
o_{1}\leq_{L(e)}w\leq_{L(e)}o_{3}

which implies
{\langle{o_{1},o_{3}}\rangle}\in R(e)
.

Claim 73.2:

\mathtt{rfr}(e)
is a strict partial order.

{claimproof}

First, the “responds before” relation is irreflexive
as a relation over all operations, and so
\mathtt{rfr}(e)
is also irreflexive.
For transitivity, assume

{\langle{o_{1},o_{2}}\rangle},{\langle{o_{2},o_{3}}\rangle}\in\mathtt{rfr}(e)
.
Clearly,
{\mathtt{method}}(o_{1})={\mathtt{method}}(o_{2})={\mathtt{method}}(o_{3})=\mathtt{read}
and
o_{1}
responds before
o_{3}
in
e
.
If we assume for contradiction
{\langle{o_{1},o_{3}}\rangle}\in(R(e)\cup R(e)^{-1})
,
we get that there exists a write operation
w
between
o_{1}
and
o_{3}
in
L(e)
.
However, due to
{\langle{o_{1},o_{2}}\rangle},{\langle{o_{2},o_{3}}\rangle}\in\mathtt{rfr}(e)
, this write operation cannot be between
o_{1}
and
o_{2}
or between
o_{2}
and
o_{3}
, which is a contradiction for any relative ordering of
o_{1}
,
o_{2}
and
o_{3}
in
L(e)
,
as the union of the segments between
o_{1}
and
o_{2}
and between
o_{2}
and
o_{3}
always contains the segment between
o_{1}
and
o_{3}
.

Now, we show that
R(e)\cup\mathtt{rfr}(e)
is a strict total order, thus inducing a sequence of operations.

Claim 73.3:

R(e)\cup\mathtt{rfr}(e)
is a strict total order over
L(e)
.

{claimproof}

Denote
R_{t}(e)\triangleq R(e)\cup\mathtt{rfr}(e)

First, we show
R_{t}(e)
is a strict (partial) order.
Irreflexivity follows from the fact that
R(e)
and
\mathtt{rfr}(e)
are irreflexive.
Transitivity follows from a simple case analysis.
Let
o_{1},o_{2},o_{3}
be operations such that

{\langle{o_{1},o_{2}}\rangle},{\langle{o_{2},o_{3}}\rangle}\in R_{t}(e)
.
There are four cases to consider:

•

If
{\langle{o_{1},o_{2}}\rangle},{\langle{o_{2},o_{3}}\rangle}\in R(e)
, then by transitivity of
R(e)
we obtain that
{\langle{o_{1},o_{3}}\rangle}\in R(e)\subseteq R_{t}(e)
.

•

Similarly, if
{\langle{o_{1},o_{2}}\rangle},{\langle{o_{2},o_{3}}\rangle}\in\mathtt{rfr}(e)
, then by transitivity of
\mathtt{rfr}(e)
we obtain that
{\langle{o_{1},o_{3}}\rangle}\in\mathtt{rfr}(e)\subseteq R_{t}(e)
.

•

If
{\langle{o_{1},o_{2}}\rangle}\in R(e)
,
{\langle{o_{2},o_{3}}\rangle}\in\mathtt{rfr}(e)
,
then there exists a write operation
w
such that

o_{1}\leq_{L(e)}w\leq_{L(e)}o_{2}
.
It must be the case that
w<_{L(e)}o_{3}
,
as otherwise
o_{3}\leq_{L(e)}w\leq_{L(e)}o_{2}
which implies
{\langle{o_{3},o_{2}}\rangle}\in R(e)
, contradicting
{\langle{o_{2},o_{3}}\rangle}\in\mathtt{rfr}(e)
.
Overall we obtain that
o_{1}\leq_{L(e)}w\leq_{L(e)}o_{3}

and thus
{\langle{o_{1},o_{3}}\rangle}\in R(e)\subseteq R_{t}(e)
.

•

If
{\langle{o_{1},o_{2}}\rangle}\in\mathtt{rfr}(e)
,
{\langle{o_{2},o_{3}}\rangle}\in R(e)
,
then there exists a write operation
w
such that

o_{2}\leq_{L(e)}w\leq_{L(e)}o_{3}
.
It must be the case that
o_{1}<_{L(e)}w
,
as otherwise
o_{2}\leq_{L(e)}w\leq_{L(e)}o_{1}
which implies
{\langle{o_{2},o_{1}}\rangle}\in R(e)
, contradicting
{\langle{o_{1},o_{2}}\rangle}\in\mathtt{rfr}(e)
.
Overall we obtain that
o_{1}\leq_{L(e)}w\leq_{L(e)}o_{3}

and thus
{\langle{o_{1},o_{3}}\rangle}\in R(e)\subseteq R_{t}(e)
.

In all cases we got that
{\langle{o_{1},o_{3}}\rangle}\in R_{t}(e)
and so
R_{t}(e)
is transitive.

Next we show
R_{t}(e)
is total. Let
o\neq o^{\prime}\in\mathsf{set}(L(e))
.
As
<_{L(e)}
is a strict total order we can assume w.l.o.g., that
o<_{L(e)}o^{\prime}
.
If
{\langle{o,o^{\prime}}\rangle}\in R(e)
, then
{\langle{o,o^{\prime}}\rangle}\in R_{t}(e)
and we are done.
Otherwise,
{\langle{o,o^{\prime}}\rangle}\notin R(e)
.
Therefore, as
o<_{L(e)}o^{\prime}
, it must be the case that
{\mathtt{method}}(o)={\mathtt{method}}(o^{\prime})=\mathtt{read}
.
From
o<_{L(e)}o^{\prime}
we can additionally deduce that
{\langle{o,o^{\prime}}\rangle}\notin<_{L(e)}^{-1}
,
and as
R(e)\subseteq<_{L(e)}
we obtain that
{\langle{o,o^{\prime}}\rangle}\notin R(e)^{-1}
.
Moreover, from
Lemma

68
we have that
o,o^{\prime}\in\mathsf{completed}(\mathsf{h}({e}))
and thus both operations have a response in
e
.
Overall
o
and
o^{\prime}
are ordered by
\mathtt{rfr}(e)
and thus ordered by
R_{t}(e)
.

We obtain a mapping
L^{\prime}:\mathsf{E}({I})\to\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}
where
L^{\prime}(e)
is the sequence over
\mathsf{set}(L(e))
induced by the strict total order
R(e)\cup\mathtt{rfr}(e)
.
For the purpose of showing
L^{\prime}
is indeed a linearization mapping with all required properties,
we first prove the following claim,
which says
L
and
L^{\prime}
agree on the ordering between write operations and operations which come after them:

Claim 73.4:

([\mathtt{write}]\mathbin{;}<_{L(e)})=([\mathtt{write}]\mathbin{;}<_{L^{\prime}(e)})

for all
e\in\mathsf{E}({I})
.

{claimproof}

By construction we have
\mathsf{set}(L(e))=\mathsf{set}(L^{\prime}(e))
.
Thus, it suffices to show that if
w,o\in\mathsf{set}(L(e))
and
{\mathtt{method}}(w)=\mathtt{write}
then
w<_{L(e)}o\iff w<_{L^{\prime}(e)}o
.

In one direction, by definition of
R(e)
we have
w<_{L(e)}o\implies{\langle{w,o}\rangle}\in R(e)\implies w<_{L^{\prime}(e)}o
.

For the converse, as
w
is a write operation,
{\langle{w,o}\rangle}\notin\mathtt{rfr}(e)

and so
w<_{L^{\prime}(e)}o\implies{\langle{w,o}\rangle}\in R(e)
.
As
R(e)\subseteq<_{L(e)}
we deduce
w<_{L(e)}o
.

Now, we show
L^{\prime}
has all required properties:

•

L^{\prime}
is a linearization mapping:

Let
e\in\mathsf{E}({I})
. First, we use
Lemma

39
to show
L^{\prime}(e)\in\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}
.
Indeed,
L(e)\in\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}
,
\mathsf{set}(L^{\prime}(e))=\mathsf{set}(L(e))
and
given any
r\in\mathsf{set}(L^{\prime}(e)|_{\mathtt{read}})
using
Section

G.2

we get that
([\mathtt{write}]\mathbin{;}<_{L(e)}\mathbin{;}[r])=([\mathtt{write}]\mathbin{;}<_{L^{\prime}(e)}\mathbin{;}[r])
,
or equivalently

\mathtt{pref}_{L^{\prime}(e)}({r})|_{\mathtt{write}}=\mathtt{pref}_{L(e)}({r})|_{\mathtt{write}}
and thus from
Lemma

39
we get
L^{\prime}(e)\in\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}
.

Now, we show
\mathsf{h}({e})\sqsubseteq L^{\prime}(e)
.
As
\mathsf{h}({e})\sqsubseteq L(e)
and by construction
\mathsf{set}(L(e))=\mathsf{set}(L^{\prime}(e))
,
using
Lemma

33
it is sufficient to show that
o<_{\mathsf{h}({e})}o^{\prime}\implies o<_{L^{\prime}(e)}o^{\prime}
for all
o,o^{\prime}\in\mathsf{set}(L^{\prime}(e))
.

We show the contra-positive: let
o,o^{\prime}\in\mathsf{set}(L^{\prime}(e))
such that

o<_{L^{\prime}(e)}o^{\prime}
does not hold.
As
<_{L^{\prime}(e)}
is total, we must have
o^{\prime}<_{L^{\prime}(e)}o
, and by construction there are two options:

–

{\langle{o^{\prime},o}\rangle}\in R(e)
:

In this case as
R(e)\subseteq<_{L(e)}
we get
o^{\prime}<_{L(e)}o
which since
L
is a legal linearization mapping implies
o<_{\mathsf{h}({e})}o^{\prime}
does not hold.

–

{\langle{o^{\prime},o}\rangle}\in\mathtt{rfr}(e)
:

In this case
o^{\prime}
responds before
o
in
e
and thus
o<_{\mathsf{h}({e})}o^{\prime}
does not hold.

•

L^{\prime}
is write-strong:

From
Section

G.2
we get that
([\mathtt{write}]\mathbin{;}<_{L(e)}\mathbin{;}[\mathtt{write}])=([\mathtt{write}]\mathbin{;}<_{L^{\prime}(e)}\mathbin{;}[\mathtt{write}])
or equivalently
L^{\prime}(e)|_{\mathtt{write}}=L(e)|_{\mathtt{write}}
for all
e\in\mathsf{E}({I})
.

Therefore, using the fact that
L
is write-strong we get
e_{1}\preceq_{\mathsf{P}}e_{2}\implies L(e_{1})|_{\mathtt{write}}\preceq_{\mathsf{P}}L(e_{2})|_{\mathtt{write}}\implies L^{\prime}(e_{1})|_{\mathtt{write}}\preceq_{\mathsf{P}}L^{\prime}(e_{2})|_{\mathtt{write}}

for all
e_{1},e_{2}\in\mathsf{E}({I})
.

•

L^{\prime}
is
\precsim^{\mathsf{w*}}
-minimal in
\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}^{\precsim_{\mathsf{P}}^{\mathsf{ww}}}(I)
:

From
Section

G.2
and the fact that by construction
\mathsf{set}(L(e))=\mathsf{set}(L^{\prime}(e))
,
we get have
L^{\prime}(e)\sim^{\mathsf{w*}}L(e)
for all
e\in\mathsf{E}({I})
and thus
L^{\prime}\sim^{\mathsf{w*}}L
.
The claim then follows from
\precsim^{\mathsf{w*}}
-minimality of
L
.

•

L^{\prime}
is decisive:

Let
e_{1},e_{2}\in\mathsf{E}({I})
be executions such that
e_{1}\preceq_{\mathsf{P}}e_{2}
.
We need to show
L^{\prime}(e_{1})\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}L^{\prime}(e_{2})
,
or equivalently
<_{L^{\prime}(e_{1})}\subseteq<_{L^{\prime}(e_{2})}
.
By definition of
L^{\prime}
, it is sufficient to prove that
R(e_{1})\subseteq R(e_{2})
and
\mathtt{rfr}(e_{1})\subseteq\mathtt{rfr}(e_{2})
.
Indeed:

–

R(e_{1})\subseteq R(e_{2})
:

Let
s_{1}=L(e_{1})\oplus(L(e_{2})|_{\mathtt{write}})
.
As
L(e_{1})\preceq_{\mathsf{P}}s_{1}
we have
(\leq_{L(e_{1})}\mathbin{;}[\mathtt{write}]\mathbin{;}\leq_{L(e_{1})})\subseteq(\leq_{s_{1}}\mathbin{;}[\mathtt{write}]\mathbin{;}\leq_{s_{1}})
.
Using
Lemma

72
, taking union over all write operations, we have

(\leq_{s_{1}}\mathbin{;}[\mathtt{write}]\mathbin{;}\leq_{s_{1}})\subseteq(\leq_{L(e_{2})}\mathbin{;}[\mathtt{write}]\mathbin{;}\leq_{L(e_{2})})
.
It follows that

R(e_{1})\subseteq R(e_{2})
.

–

\mathtt{rfr}(e_{1})\subseteq\mathtt{rfr}(e_{2})
:

Let
{\langle{o,o^{\prime}}\rangle}\in\mathtt{rfr}(e_{1})
.
Then
{\mathtt{method}}(o)={\mathtt{method}}(o^{\prime})=\mathtt{read}
and
o
responds before
o^{\prime}
in
e_{1}
.
As
e_{1}\preceq_{\mathsf{P}}e_{2}
this is also the case in
e_{2}
.
It remains to show that
{\langle{o,o^{\prime}}\rangle}\notin(R(e_{2})\cup R(e_{2})^{-1})
.
Assume otherwise.
If
{\langle{o,o^{\prime}}\rangle}\in R(e_{2})
, then there exists a write operation
w\in\mathsf{set}(L(e_{2}))
such that
o\leq_{L(e_{2})}w\leq_{L(e_{2})}o^{\prime}
.
From
Lemma

72
we have

(\leq_{L(e_{2})}\mathbin{;}[w]\mathbin{;}\leq_{L(e_{2})})\cap(\mathsf{set}(s_{1})\times\mathsf{set}(s_{1}))\subseteq(\leq_{s_{1}}\mathbin{;}[w]\mathbin{;}\leq_{s_{1}})

where
s_{1}=L(e_{1})\oplus(L(e_{2})|_{\mathtt{write}})
.
As
o,o^{\prime}
have a response in
e_{1}
we have
o,o^{\prime}\in\mathsf{set}(L(e_{1}))\subseteq\mathsf{set}(s_{1})
, and we obtain that

{\langle{o,o^{\prime}}\rangle}\in(\leq_{s_{1}}\mathbin{;}[w]\mathbin{;}\leq_{s_{1}})
.
That is,
o\leq_{s_{1}}w\leq_{s_{1}}o^{\prime}
.
As
L(e_{1})\preceq_{\mathsf{P}}s_{1}

and
o^{\prime}\in\mathsf{set}(L(e_{1}))
we must have
o\leq_{L(e_{1})}w\leq_{L(e_{1})}o^{\prime}

and thus
{\langle{o,o^{\prime}}\rangle}\in R(e_{1})
,
contradicting
{\langle{o,o^{\prime}}\rangle}\in\mathtt{rfr}(e_{1})
.

The case where
{\langle{o,o^{\prime}}\rangle}\in R(e_{2})^{-1}
similarly leads to
{\langle{o,o^{\prime}}\rangle}\in R(e_{1})^{-1}
, which is again a contradiction. ∎

G.3
Minimal Linearizations for Decisive Registers

Using the notation we developed, every
\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}^{\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}}(I)
is the set of decisive linearization of the implementation
I
, and we have:

I\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{d}}\iff\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}^{\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}}(I)\neq\emptyset

As shown in
Lemma

73
, every write strong implementation is decisive.
Fittingly, for decisive implementations we use a weaker notion of minimality.
Specifically, we can get
\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}
-minimal linearizations:

Lemma 74
.

If
I\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{d}}
, then
\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}^{\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}}(I)
contains a
\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}
-minimal linearization.

Proof.

We apply
Corollary

61
, using the fact that

\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}
is a well-founded partial order on
\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}
.
∎

As already shown in the write strong case, minimal linearizations contain no pending reads:

Lemma 75
.

If
I\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{d}}
and
L\in\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}^{\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}}(I)
is
\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}
-minimal,
then
L
is pending-read-free.

Proof.

We need to show
\mathsf{set}(L(e)|_{\mathtt{read}})\subseteq\mathsf{set}(\mathsf{completed}(\mathsf{h}({e})))
for every
e\in\mathsf{E}({I})
.

We proceed by induction on
\left|{e}\right|
. For
e=\varepsilon
we have by definition of linearizability that
L(e)=\varepsilon
, and we are done.

Let
e
be an execution of length
\left|{e}\right|=n
.
As in the proof of
Lemma

68
, it is sufficient to show that
s=L(e)
,
where

s=L(e)|_{\mathsf{set}(L(e))\setminus\left(\mathsf{set}(L(e)|_{\mathtt{read}})\setminus\mathsf{set}(\mathsf{completed}(\mathsf{h}({e})))\right)}
is the subsequence of
L(e)
where every pending read operation is removed.

We wish to apply
Lemma

62
. We have:

•

From
Lemma

38
we have that
s\in\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}
and from
Lemma

34
we get
\mathsf{h}({e})\sqsubseteq s
.

•

L(e^{\prime})\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}s
for all
e^{\prime}\in\mathsf{E}({I})
such that
e^{\prime}\precneqq_{\mathsf{P}}e
:

As
L
is decisive we have
L(e^{\prime})\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}L(e)
.
By definition we also have
s\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}L(e)
.
Therefore, it is sufficient to show that
\mathsf{set}(L(e^{\prime}))\subseteq\mathsf{set}(s)
.

Let
o\in\mathsf{set}(L(e^{\prime}))

–

if
{\mathtt{method}}(o)=\mathtt{write}
then from
L(e^{\prime})\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}L(e)
we have
o\in\mathsf{set}(L(e)|_{\mathtt{write}})
and as
L(e)|_{\mathtt{write}}=s|_{\mathtt{write}}
we get
o\in\mathsf{set}(s)
.

–

If
{\mathtt{method}}(o)=\mathtt{read}
then due to our inductive assumption (applied to the strictly smaller
e^{\prime}
) we have
o\in\mathsf{set}(\mathsf{completed}(\mathsf{h}({e^{\prime}})))
.

As
\mathsf{set}(\mathsf{completed}(\mathsf{h}({e^{\prime}})))\subseteq\mathsf{set}(\mathsf{completed}(\mathsf{h}({e})))
we deduce that
o\in\mathsf{set}(\mathsf{completed}(\mathsf{h}({e})))
and thus by construction
o\in\mathsf{set}(s)
.

•

s\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}L(e^{\prime})
for all
e^{\prime}\in\mathsf{E}({I})
such that
e\precneqq_{\mathsf{P}}e^{\prime}
:

Follows from
s\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}L(e)\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}L(e^{\prime})
, where the former is by definition, and the latter is from
L
being decisive.

Overall, we can apply
Lemma

62
and get that
s=L(e)
, as the equivalence induced by
\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}
is equality.
∎

Lemmas

77
and
78
are stated in a way which applies both to the set of write strong linearizations and to the set of decisive linearizations.
To enable this simultaneous argument for both cases, we first show that
\precsim^{\mathsf{w*}}
-minimal linearizations are also
\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}
-minimal:

Lemma 76
.

If
I\in\mathcal{I}_{\mathsf{ws}}
and

L
is
\precsim^{\mathsf{w*}}
-minimal in
\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}^{\precsim_{\mathsf{P}}^{\mathsf{ww}}}(I)
,
then it is also
\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}
-minimal in
\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}^{\precsim_{\mathsf{P}}^{\mathsf{ww}}}(I)

Proof.

Assume otherwise and let
L^{\prime}\in\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}^{\precsim_{\mathsf{P}}^{\mathsf{ww}}}(I)
be a linearization mapping such that
L^{\prime}\precneqq_{\mathsf{S}}L
.
Then, there exist an execution
e\in\mathsf{E}({I})
and a (possibly pending) operation
o\in\mathsf{h}({e})
such that
L^{\prime}(e)\precneqq_{\mathsf{S}}L(e)
and
\tilde{o}\in\mathsf{set}(L(e))\setminus\mathsf{set}(L^{\prime}(e))
,
where
\tilde{o}
is
o
extended to a completed operation (if necessary).
Consider the following cases:

•

If
o
is a read operation, from
\tilde{o}\notin\mathsf{set}(L^{\prime}(e))
we deduce that
o
is pending.
As
\tilde{o}\in\mathsf{set}(L(e))
, this contradicts
Lemma

68

•

If
o
is a write operation,
note that due to
Lemma

64
lifted point-wise we obtain from
L^{\prime}\precneqq_{\mathsf{S}}L
that
L^{\prime}\precsim^{\mathsf{w*}}L
.
Since
L
is
\precsim^{\mathsf{w*}}
-minimal in
\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}^{\precsim_{\mathsf{P}}^{\mathsf{ww}}}(I)
and
L^{\prime}\in\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}^{\precsim_{\mathsf{P}}^{\mathsf{ww}}}(I)
we deduce that

L^{\prime}\sim^{\mathsf{w*}}L
and in particular
L^{\prime}(e)\sim^{\mathsf{w*}}L(e)
.
Applying
Lemma

65
this implies that
L^{\prime}(e)|_{\mathtt{write}}=L(e)|_{\mathtt{write}}
,
which contradicts
\tilde{o}\in\mathsf{set}(L(e)|_{\mathtt{write}})\setminus\mathsf{set}(L^{\prime}(e)|_{\mathtt{write}})
.

In both cases we reach a contradiction and the lemma follows.
∎

Lemmas

77
and
78
now refer to a decisive linearization that is
\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}
-minimal in
\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}^{R}(I)
.
They can be applied both to the write-strong case (by taking
R=\precsim^{\mathsf{w*}}
and using
Lemma

73
and
Lemma

76
) and to the decisive case (by taking
R=\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}
).
For both choices it can be easily verified that
R
satisfies the conditions required by the lemmas.

The first property we prove in this way is that minimal linearizations are response-activated.
(i.e., we do not add additional operations to the linearization when extending an execution with an additional transition,
unless that transition is a response.)

Lemma 77
.

If
I
is an implementation and

L\in\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}^{R}(I)
is
\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}
-minimal and decisive,
where
R
is reflexive,
then
L
is response-activated.

Proof.

Let
e\in\mathsf{E}({I})
and
t\in I.{\mathtt{T}}
such that
e\cdot t\in\mathsf{E}({I})
and
t\notin\mathsf{R}
.
We show
L(e)=L(e\cdot t)
.
We wish to apply
Lemma

62
to
e\cdot t
and
L(e)
.
Indeed, we have the following:

1.

L(e)\in\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}
:

Immediate from
L
being a valid linearization mapping.

2.

\mathsf{h}({e\cdot t})\sqsubseteq L(e)
:

Follows from
Lemma

35
and
\mathsf{h}({e})\sqsubseteq L(e)
.

3.

L(e)\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}L(e\cdot t)
:

Follows from
L
being decisive.

4.

{\langle{L(e^{\prime}),L(e)}\rangle}\in R
for all
e^{\prime}\in\mathsf{E}({I})
such that
e^{\prime}\precneqq_{\mathsf{P}}e\cdot t
:

e^{\prime}\precneqq_{\mathsf{P}}e\cdot t\implies e^{\prime}\preceq_{\mathsf{P}}e
and so this follows from the fact that
L
respects
R
.

5.

{\langle{L(e),L(e^{\prime})}\rangle}\in R
for all
e^{\prime}\in\mathsf{E}({I})
such that
e\cdot t\precneqq_{\mathsf{P}}e^{\prime}
:

e\cdot t\precneqq_{\mathsf{P}}e^{\prime}\implies e\preceq_{\mathsf{P}}e^{\prime}
and so this follows from the fact that
L
respects
R
.

From
Lemma

62
we deduce that
L(e)=L(e\cdot t)
,
as the equivalence induced by the partial order
\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}
is equality.
∎

Next, we show that minimal linearizations are last-completed.
(i.e., the last operation in a minimal linearization is some operation that is completed in the linearized execution.)

Lemma 78
.

If
I
is an implementation,
L\in\mathscr{L}_{\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}}^{R}(I)
is
\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}
-minimal and decisive,
and
R
is a relation such that:

1.

{\langle{s_{1},s_{2}\cdot o}\rangle}\in R\land o\notin s_{1}\implies{\langle{s_{1},s_{2}}\rangle}\in R
for every
s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}
and operation
o
.

2.

\preceq_{\mathsf{P}}\subseteq R
.

3.

R
is transitive.

then
L
is last-completed.

Proof.

We need to show
\mathsf{last_{op}}(L(e))\in\mathsf{set}(\mathsf{completed}(\mathsf{h}({e})))

for all
e\in\mathsf{E}({I})
such that
L(e)\neq\varepsilon
.
Assume for contradiction this is false, and let
e
be an execution of minimal size such that
\mathsf{last_{op}}(L(e))\neq\varepsilon
and
\mathsf{last_{op}}(L(e))\notin\mathsf{set}(\mathsf{completed}(\mathsf{h}({e})))
.
Denote
o\triangleq\mathsf{last_{op}}(L(e))
.
Let
s=\mathtt{pref}_{L(e)}({o})
be the prefix of
L(e)
with
o
removed.
From
\preceq_{\mathsf{P}}\subseteq R
we get that
R
is reflexive.
We wish to use
Lemma

62
.
We have:

1.

s\in\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}
:

Follows from
s\preceq_{\mathsf{P}}L(e)\in\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}
and
\mathsf{Spec}_{\mathsf{Reg}}
being prefix closed.

2.

\mathsf{h}({e})\sqsubseteq s
:

Follows from
Lemma

34
using
\mathsf{h}({e})\sqsubseteq L(e)

3.

s\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}L(e)
:

Holds since
s\preceq_{\mathsf{P}}L(e)
.

4.

{\langle{L(e^{\prime}),s}\rangle}\in R
for all
e^{\prime}\in\mathsf{E}({I})
such that
e^{\prime}\precneqq_{\mathsf{P}}e
:

As
{\langle{L(e^{\prime}),L(e)}\rangle}\in R
,
using the first assumption on
R
it is sufficient to show that
o\notin L(e^{\prime})
.
Indeed, if we assume for contradiction
o\in L(e^{\prime})
then since
\mathsf{set}(\mathsf{completed}(\mathsf{h}({e^{\prime}})))\subseteq\mathsf{set}(\mathsf{completed}(\mathsf{h}({e})))

we deduce that
o\notin\mathsf{set}(\mathsf{completed}(\mathsf{h}({e^{\prime}})))
.
From
L(e^{\prime})\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}L(e)
,
o\in L(e^{\prime})
,and
o=\mathsf{last_{op}}(L(e))
we can deduce that
o=\mathsf{last_{op}}(L(e^{\prime}))

and thus
\mathsf{last_{op}}(L(e^{\prime}))\notin\mathsf{set}(\mathsf{completed}(\mathsf{h}({e^{\prime}})))
,
contradicting the minimality of
e
.
From this contradiction we deduce that
L(e^{\prime})\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}s

5.

{\langle{s,L(e^{\prime})}\rangle}\in R
for all
e^{\prime}\in\mathsf{E}({I})
such that
e\precneqq_{\mathsf{P}}e^{\prime}
:

We have that
s\preceq_{\mathsf{P}}L(e)

and so due to our assumptions
{\langle{s,L(e)}\rangle}\in R
.
As
{\langle{L(e),L(e^{\prime})}\rangle}\in R

we obtain from transitivity that

{\langle{s,L(e^{\prime})}\rangle}\in R
.

From
Lemma

62
we have
s=L(e)
, as the equivalence induced by
\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}
is equality.
This is a contradiction, and so the lemma holds.
∎

G.4
Conclusion

We obtain the existence of the lazy linearizations required for
Appendix

F
:

Proof Of
Thm.

50
.

From
Lemma

73
we obtain a
\precsim^{\mathsf{w*}}
-minimal mapping which is write strong and decisive.
It is also pending-read-free due to
Lemma

68
.
Using
Lemma

76
,
we have that the mapping is also
\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}
-minimal.
Therefore, it is response-activated due to
Lemma

77
and last-completed due to
Lemma

78
.
∎

Proof Of
Thm.

51
.

From
Lemma

74
we obtain a
\preceq_{\mathsf{S}}
-minimal mapping which is decisive.
It is pending-read free due to
Lemma

75
,
response-activated due to
Lemma

77
,
and last-completed due to
Lemma

78
.
∎

Appendix H
ABD

Now we formally define multi-writer
\mathtt{ABD}
.
First we need some notation:

1.

\mathsf{TS}={\mathbb{N}}\times(\mathsf{Tid}\cup\{{\bot}\})
(The timestamps used in ABD, composed of an integer and a thread id.
\mathsf{Tid}
is a finite and ordered set, and
\bot
is defined as smaller than all thread ids).

2.

\mathsf{Msg}=\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{}}\{{\mathtt{query}(p,k)\;|\;p\in\mathsf{Tid},k\in\mathsf{Id}}\}\cup\\
\{{\mathtt{update}(p,k,{\langle{v,\mathit{ts}}\rangle})\;|\;p\in\mathsf{Tid},k\in\mathsf{Id},v\in\mathsf{Val},\mathit{ts}\in\mathsf{TS}}\}\end{array}

3.

\mathsf{Acks}=(\mathsf{Tid}\times(\mathsf{Val}\times\mathsf{TS}))\cup\mathsf{Tid}

4.

\mathit{PC}_{\mathtt{ABD}}=\{{\mathtt{main},\mathtt{w_{b}},\mathtt{w_{q}},\mathtt{w_{u}},\mathtt{w_{e}},\mathtt{r_{b}},\mathtt{r_{q}},\mathtt{r_{u}},\mathtt{r_{e}}}\}

We define order on timestamps and lift it to order on pairs in
\mathsf{Val}\times\mathsf{TS}
:

\displaystyle{\langle{t_{1},p_{1}}\rangle}<{\langle{t_{2},p_{2}}\rangle}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow t_{1}<t_{2}\lor(t_{1}=t_{2}\land p_{1}<p_{2})

\displaystyle{\langle{v_{1},\mathit{ts}_{1}}\rangle}<{\langle{v_{2},\mathit{ts}_{2}}\rangle}

\displaystyle\Longleftrightarrow\mathit{ts}_{1}<\mathit{ts}_{2}

Now, we define the LTS:

•

\mathtt{ABD}.{\mathtt{Q}}=(\mathsf{Msg}\rightharpoonup\mathcal{P}({\mathsf{Acks}}))\times(\mathsf{Tid}\to\mathsf{Id})\times(\mathsf{Tid}\to(\mathsf{Val}\times\mathsf{TS}))\times(\mathsf{Tid}\to\mathit{PC}_{\mathtt{ABD}})\times(\mathsf{Tid}\to\mathsf{Val})
(mapping from messages to acks, used operation IDs, most recent value and timestamp known, program counter, input/output argument)

•

\mathtt{ABD}.{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}=\mathsf{I}\mathsf{R}(\mathsf{Reg})\cup\{{\mathtt{int}{\langle{p}\rangle}\;|\;p\in\mathsf{Tid}}\}

•

\mathtt{ABD}.{\mathtt{q_{\mathtt{0}}}}={\langle{\emptyset,\lambda p\ldotp 0,\lambda p\ldotp\mathtt{main},\lambda p\ldotp{\langle{0,{\langle{0,0}\rangle}}\rangle},\lambda p\ldotp 0}\rangle}

•

The transitions are as follows:

\frac{a=\mathtt{inv}{\langle{\mathtt{read},\bot,p,k}\rangle}\quad\quad\mathit{PC}(p)=\mathtt{main}\quad k\notin ID(p)}{{\langle{M,ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC},\mathit{A}}\rangle}{}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.8pt}{$\xrightarrow{a}$}}{}{\langle{M,ID[p\mapsto ID(p)\cdot k],\mathit{V},\mathit{PC}[p\mapsto\mathtt{r_{b}}],\mathit{A}}\rangle}}\quad\textsc{{read-inv}}\frac{\mathit{PC}(p)=\mathtt{r_{b}}\quad k=ID(p)[\mathsf{last}]\quad\quad m=\mathtt{query}(p,k)}{{\langle{M,ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC},\mathit{A}}\rangle}{}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.8pt}{$\xrightarrow{\mathtt{int}{\langle{p}\rangle}}$}}{}{\langle{M[m\mapsto\emptyset],ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC}[p\mapsto\mathtt{r_{q}}],\mathit{A}}\rangle}}\quad\textsc{{read-query}}\frac{\mathit{PC}(p)=\mathtt{r_{q}}\quad k=ID(p)[\mathsf{last}]\quad\quad Q\subseteq\{{p\;|\;{\langle{p,\_}\rangle}\in M(\mathtt{query}(p,k))}\}\quad\left|{Q}\right|>\frac{\left|{\mathsf{Tid}}\right|}{2}\quad\quad{\langle{v_{\mathit{m}},\mathit{ts}_{\mathit{m}}}\rangle}=\max\{{{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}\;|\;\exists p^{\prime}\in Q\ldotp{\langle{p^{\prime},{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}}\rangle}\in M(\mathtt{query}(p,k))}\}\quad\quad m=\mathtt{update}(p,k,{\langle{v_{\mathit{m}},\mathit{ts}_{\mathit{m}}}\rangle})}{{\langle{M,ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC},\mathit{A}}\rangle}{}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.8pt}{$\xrightarrow{\mathtt{int}{\langle{p}\rangle}}$}}{}{\langle{M[m\mapsto\emptyset],ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC}[p\mapsto\mathtt{r_{u}}],\mathit{A}[p\mapsto v_{\mathit{m}}]}\rangle}}\quad\textsc{{read-update}}\frac{\mathit{PC}(p)=\mathtt{r_{u}}\quad k=ID(p)[\mathsf{last}]\quad\quad\left|{M(\mathtt{update}(p,k,\_)}\right|>\frac{\left|{\mathsf{Tid}}\right|}{2}}{{\langle{M,ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC},\mathit{A}}\rangle}{}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.8pt}{$\xrightarrow{\mathtt{int}{\langle{p}\rangle}}$}}{}{\langle{M,ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC}[p\mapsto\mathtt{r_{e}}],\mathit{A}}\rangle}}\quad\textsc{{read-end}}\frac{a=\mathtt{res}{\langle{\mathtt{read},\mathit{A}(p),p,k}\rangle}\quad\quad\mathit{PC}(p)=\mathtt{r_{e}}\quad k=ID(p)[\mathsf{last}]}{{\langle{M,ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC},\mathit{A}}\rangle}{}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.8pt}{$\xrightarrow{a}$}}{}{\langle{M,ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC}[p\mapsto\mathtt{main}],\mathit{A}[p\mapsto 0]}\rangle}}\quad\textsc{{read-res}}\frac{a=\mathtt{inv}{\langle{\mathtt{write},v_{\text{in}},p,k}\rangle}\quad\quad\mathit{PC}(p)=\mathtt{main}\quad k\notin ID(p)}{{\langle{M,ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC},\mathit{A}}\rangle}{}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.8pt}{$\xrightarrow{a}$}}{}{\langle{M,ID[p\mapsto ID(p)\cdot k],\mathit{V},\mathit{PC}[p\mapsto\mathtt{w_{b}}],\mathit{A}[p\mapsto v_{\text{in}}]}\rangle}}\quad\textsc{{write-inv}}\frac{\mathit{PC}(p)=\mathtt{w_{b}}\quad k=ID(p)[\mathsf{last}]\quad\quad m=\mathtt{query}(p,k)}{{\langle{M,ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC},\mathit{A}}\rangle}{}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.8pt}{$\xrightarrow{\mathtt{int}{\langle{p}\rangle}}$}}{}{\langle{M[m\mapsto\emptyset],ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC}[p\mapsto\mathtt{w_{q}}],\mathit{A}}\rangle}}\quad\textsc{{write-query}}\frac{\mathit{PC}(p)=\mathtt{w_{q}}\quad k=ID(p)[\mathsf{last}]\quad\quad Q\subseteq\{{p\;|\;{\langle{p,\_}\rangle}\in M(\mathtt{query}(p,k))}\}\quad\left|{Q}\right|>\frac{\left|{\mathsf{Tid}}\right|}{2}\quad\quad{\langle{t,\_}\rangle}=\max\{{\mathit{ts}^{\prime}\;|\;\exists p^{\prime}\in Q,v^{\prime}\ldotp{\langle{p^{\prime},{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}}\rangle}\in M(\mathtt{query}(p,k))}\}\quad\quad m=\mathtt{update}(p,k,{\langle{\mathit{A}(p),{\langle{t+1,p}\rangle}}\rangle}}{{\langle{M,ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC},\mathit{A}}\rangle}{}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.8pt}{$\xrightarrow{\mathtt{int}{\langle{p}\rangle}}$}}{}{\langle{M[m\mapsto\emptyset],ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC}[p\mapsto\mathtt{w_{u}}],\mathit{A}}\rangle}}\quad\textsc{{write-update}}\frac{\mathit{PC}(p)=\mathtt{w_{u}}\quad k=ID(p)[\mathsf{last}]\quad\quad\left|{M(\mathtt{update}(p,k,\_)}\right|>\frac{\left|{\mathsf{Tid}}\right|}{2}}{{\langle{M,ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC},\mathit{A}}\rangle}{}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.8pt}{$\xrightarrow{\mathtt{int}{\langle{p}\rangle}}$}}{}{\langle{M,ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC}[p\mapsto\mathtt{w_{e}}],\mathit{A}}\rangle}}\quad\textsc{{write-end}}\frac{a=\mathtt{res}{\langle{\mathtt{write},\bot,p,k}\rangle}\quad\quad\mathit{PC}(p)=\mathtt{w_{e}}\quad k=ID(p)[\mathsf{last}]}{{\langle{M,ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC},\mathit{A}}\rangle}{}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.8pt}{$\xrightarrow{a}$}}{}{\langle{M,ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC}[p\mapsto\mathtt{main}],\mathit{A}[p\mapsto 0]}\rangle}}\quad\textsc{{write-res}}\frac{m=\mathtt{query}(\_,\_)\in\textsf{dom}{({M})}\quad\quad\forall{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}.{\langle{p,{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}}\rangle}\notin M(m)\quad\quad M^{\prime}=M[m\mapsto M(m)\uplus\{{{\langle{p,\mathit{V}(p)}\rangle}}\}]}{{\langle{M,ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC},\mathit{A}}\rangle}{}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.8pt}{$\xrightarrow{\mathtt{int}{\langle{p}\rangle}}$}}{}{\langle{M^{\prime},ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC},\mathit{A}}\rangle}}\quad\textsc{{server-query-ack}}\frac{m=\mathtt{update}(\_,\_,{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle})\in\textsf{dom}{({M})}\quad\quad M^{\prime}=M[m\mapsto M(m)\uplus\{{p}\}]\quad\quad\mathit{V}^{\prime}=\mathit{V}[p\mapsto\max\{{\mathit{V}(p),{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}}\}]}{{\langle{M,ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC},\mathit{A}}\rangle}{}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.8pt}{$\xrightarrow{\mathtt{int}{\langle{p}\rangle}}$}}{}{\langle{M^{\prime},ID,\mathit{V}^{\prime},\mathit{PC},\mathit{A}}\rangle}}\quad\textsc{{server-update-ack}}

We show
\mathtt{ABD}
can be simulated by
\mathtt{DR}
, and it follows that it is decisively linearizable.

Theorem 79
.

\mathtt{ABD}\sqsubseteq_{\mathsf{F}}\mathtt{DR}

Proof.

First, we define the following notations:

•

Define
R_{\mathit{pc}}\subseteq\mathit{PC}_{\mathtt{ABD}}\times\mathit{PC}_{\text{D}}
by

R_{\mathit{pc}}=\{{{\langle{\mathtt{main},\mathtt{main}}\rangle},{\langle{\mathtt{r_{b}},\mathtt{r_{b}}}\rangle},{\langle{\mathtt{r_{q}},\mathtt{r_{l}}}\rangle},{\langle{\mathtt{r_{u}},\mathtt{r_{l}}}\rangle},{\langle{\mathtt{r_{u}},\mathtt{r_{e}}}\rangle},{\langle{\mathtt{r_{e}},\mathtt{r_{e}}}\rangle},{\langle{\mathtt{w_{b}},\mathtt{w_{b}}}\rangle},{\langle{\mathtt{w_{q}},\mathtt{w_{w}}}\rangle},{\langle{\mathtt{w_{u}},\mathtt{w_{w}}}\rangle},{\langle{\mathtt{w_{u}},\mathtt{w_{e}}}\rangle},{\langle{\mathtt{w_{e}},\mathtt{w_{e}}}\rangle}}\}

•

Given a mapping
M:\mathsf{Msg}\rightharpoonup\mathcal{P}({\mathsf{Acks}})
define

H(M)=\{{{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}\;|\;\exists m=\mathtt{update}(\_,\_,{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle})\in\textsf{dom}{({M})}\ldotp\left|{M(m)}\right|>\frac{\left|{\mathsf{Tid}}\right|}{2}}\}\cup\{{{\langle{0,{\langle{0,0}\rangle}}\rangle}}\}

The simulation relation is defined by

{\langle{q,{q}^{\#}}\rangle}={\langle{{\langle{M,ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC},\mathit{A}}\rangle},{\langle{v,\mathit{Ver},\ell,{ID}^{\#},{\mathit{PC}}^{\#},\mathit{VS},\mathit{S},{\mathit{A}}^{\#}}\rangle}}\rangle}\in R
iff the following conditions hold:

Conditions on
q
:

(R1)

For every
m=\mathtt{update}(\_,\_,{\langle{v,\mathit{ts}}\rangle})\in\textsf{dom}{({M})}
and
p^{\prime}\in M(m)
we have
\mathit{V}(p)\geq{\langle{v,\mathit{ts}}\rangle}

(R2)

For every
Q\subseteq\mathsf{Tid}
such that
\left|{Q}\right|>\frac{\left|{\mathsf{Tid}}\right|}{2}
we have
\max\{{\mathit{V}(p)\;|\;p\in Q}\}\geq\max H(M)

(R3)

For every
p\in\mathsf{Tid}
we have:

(R3a)

If
k\notin ID(p)
, or
k=ID(p)[\mathsf{last}]
with
\mathit{PC}(p)\in\{{\mathtt{r_{b}},\mathtt{w_{b}},\mathtt{r_{q}},\mathtt{w_{q}}}\}
, then there exists no pair
{\langle{v,\mathit{ts}}\rangle}
such that
\mathtt{update}(p,k,{\langle{v,\mathit{ts}}\rangle})\in\textsf{dom}{({M})}

(R3b)

If
k=ID(p)[\mathsf{last}]
and
\mathit{PC}(p)\in\{{\mathtt{w_{u}},\mathtt{r_{u}}}\}
then there exists a single pair, denoted
{\langle{v_{p},\mathit{ts}_{p}}\rangle}
in the following, such that
m=\mathtt{update}(p,k,{\langle{v_{p},\mathit{ts}_{p}}\rangle})\in\textsf{dom}{({M})}
and
v_{p}=\mathit{A}(p)
.
If
\mathit{PC}(p)=\mathtt{w_{u}}
then
\mathit{ts}_{p}={\langle{\_,p}\rangle}

(R4)

For every pair
{\langle{v,{\langle{t,p}\rangle}}\rangle}
in the set

\{{{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}\;|\;\mathtt{update}(\_,\_,{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle})\in\textsf{dom}{({M})}}\}\cup\\
\{{{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}\;|\;m=\mathtt{query}(\_,\_)\in\textsf{dom}{({M})}\land{\langle{\_,{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}}\rangle}\in M(m)}\}\cup\{{\mathit{V}(p^{\prime})\;|\;p^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Tid}}\}

such that
{\langle{v,{\langle{t,p}\rangle}}\rangle}\notin H(M)
it holds that

\mathit{PC}(p)=\mathtt{w_{u}}
and
{\langle{v,{\langle{t,p}\rangle}}\rangle}={\langle{v_{p},\mathit{ts}_{p}}\rangle}

Conditions on
{q}^{\#}
:

5.

\ell=0

6.

\mathit{Ver}\geq 0

7.

For all
p\in\mathsf{Tid}
,
\mathit{S}(p)\leq\mathit{Ver}

Conditions on
{\langle{q,{q}^{\#}}\rangle}
: There exists a monotonically increasing function
f:\{{0{,}\ldots{,}\mathit{Ver}}\}\to\mathsf{Val}\times\mathsf{TS}
with
f(0)={\langle{0,{\langle{0,0}\rangle}}\rangle}
such that the following holds:

8.

ID={ID}^{\#}

9.

f(\mathit{Ver})=\max H(M)={\langle{v,\_}\rangle}
.

10.

For every
p\in\mathsf{Tid}
:

(R10a)

{\langle{\mathit{PC}(p),{\mathit{PC}}^{\#}(p)}\rangle}\in R_{\mathit{pc}}

(R10b)

If
{\mathit{PC}}^{\#}(p)\in\{{\mathtt{r_{e}},\mathtt{w_{b}},\mathtt{w_{w}}}\}
then
\mathit{A}(p)={\mathit{A}}^{\#}(p)
.

(R10c)

If
{\mathit{PC}}^{\#}(p)=\mathtt{r_{l}}
then

\mathit{VS}(p)=\{{v^{\prime}\;|\;\exists\mathit{ts}^{\prime}\ldotp f(\mathit{S}(p))\leq{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}\leq f(\mathit{Ver})\land{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}\in H(M)}\}

(R10d)

If
\mathit{PC}(p)\in\{{\mathtt{r_{q}},\mathtt{w_{q}}}\}
then denoting
m=\mathtt{query}(p,k)
where
k=ID(p)[\mathsf{last}]
, for every
Q\subseteq\{{p^{\prime}\;|\;{\langle{p^{\prime},\_}\rangle}\in M(m)}\}
and
Q^{\prime}\subseteq\mathsf{Tid}\setminus Q
with
\left|{Q\uplus Q^{\prime}}\right|>\frac{\left|{\mathsf{Tid}}\right|}{2}
we have

\max\{{{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}\;|\;\exists p^{\prime}\in Q\ldotp{\langle{p^{\prime},{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}}\rangle}\in M(m)}\}\cup\{{\mathit{V}(p^{\prime})\;|\;p^{\prime}\in Q^{\prime}}\}\geq f(\mathit{S}(p))

(R10e)

If
\mathit{PC}(p)\in\{{\mathtt{r_{u}},\mathtt{w_{u}}}\}
and
{\langle{v_{p},\mathit{ts}_{p}}\rangle}\notin H(M)
then
f(\mathit{S}(p))\leq{\langle{v_{p},\mathit{ts}_{p}}\rangle}
and
{\mathit{PC}}^{\#}(p)\in\{{\mathtt{r_{l}},\mathtt{w_{w}}}\}
.

(R10f)

If
\mathit{PC}(p)\in\{{\mathtt{r_{u}},\mathtt{w_{u}}}\}
and
{\langle{v_{p},\mathit{ts}_{p}}\rangle}\in H(M)
then
{\mathit{PC}}^{\#}(p)\in\{{\mathtt{r_{e}},\mathtt{w_{e}}}\}
.

Clearly, for the initial states we have
{\langle{\mathtt{ABD}.{\mathtt{q_{\mathtt{0}}}},\mathtt{DR}.{\mathtt{q_{\mathtt{0}}}}}\rangle}\in R
.

Given states
{\langle{q,{q}^{\#}}\rangle}={\langle{{\langle{M,ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC},\mathit{A}}\rangle},{\langle{v,\mathit{Ver},\ell,{ID}^{\#},{\mathit{PC}}^{\#},\mathit{VS},\mathit{S},{\mathit{A}}^{\#}}\rangle}}\rangle}\in R
,
a monotonic function
f
such that the above conditions hold,
and a transition
{\langle{M,ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC},\mathit{A}}\rangle}{}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.8pt}{$\xrightarrow{a}$}_{\mathtt{ABD}}}{}q^{\prime}={\langle{M^{\prime},\_,\_,\_,\_}\rangle}
, we present the following:

1.

Proof that
q^{\prime}
upholds the necessary conditions.

2.

A sequence of transitions

{\langle{v,\mathit{Ver},\ell,{ID}^{\#},{\mathit{PC}}^{\#},\mathit{VS},\mathit{S},{\mathit{A}}^{\#}}\rangle}{}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.8pt}{${\mathrel{\to}^{\bar{a}}}$}_{\mathtt{DR}}}{}{{q}^{\#}}^{\prime}={\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{Ver}^{\prime},\_,\_,\_,\_,\_,\_}\rangle}
such that
a|_{\mathsf{I}\mathsf{R}(\mathsf{Reg})}=\bar{a}|_{\mathsf{I}\mathsf{R}(\mathsf{Reg})}
, and a proof that
{{q}^{\#}}^{\prime}
upholds necessary conditions.

3.

A proof using a function
f^{\prime}:\{{0{,}\ldots{,}\mathit{Ver}^{\prime}}\}\to\mathsf{Val}\times\mathsf{TS}
that the pair
{\langle{q^{\prime},{{q}^{\#}}^{\prime}}\rangle}
upholds necessary conditions.

Denote
H=H(M)
,
H^{\prime}=H(M^{\prime})
.

When all values relevant for certain items in the list of conditions are unchanged between
{\langle{q,{{q}^{\#}}}\rangle}
and
{\langle{q^{\prime},{{q}^{\#}}^{\prime}}\rangle}
, they trivially still hold, and we omit their justification.

In particular, for all transition rules except possibly for
server-update-ack
and
write-update
we will have
\mathit{Ver}^{\prime}=\mathit{Ver}
,
v^{\prime}=v
take
f^{\prime}=f
, and it is easy to check that
H^{\prime}=H
, so there is no need to justify
Item

9
.

We also omit the justification of
Item

1010a
as it is easily verifiable given
{\langle{q^{\prime},{{q}^{\#}}^{\prime}}\rangle}
,
and justification for conditions that clearly hold vacuously.

In the following we only mention conditions that require extra justification.

We consider all cases for the transition rule used:

•

read-inv
:

Here
a=\mathtt{inv}{\langle{\mathtt{read},\bot,p,k}\rangle}
,
q^{\prime}={\langle{M,ID[p\mapsto ID(p)\cdot k],\mathit{V},\mathit{PC}[p\mapsto\mathtt{r_{b}}],\mathit{A}}\rangle}
,
and we have preconditions
\mathit{PC}(p)=\mathtt{main}
and
k\notin ID(p)
.

For
Item

33a
,
k
is required to satisfy it as
\mathit{PC}[p\mapsto\mathtt{r_{b}}](p)=\mathtt{r_{b}}
, and indeed we can deduce this from
k\notin ID(p)
.

We have that
{ID}^{\#}=ID
which implies
k\notin{ID}^{\#}(p)
, and
{\mathit{PC}}^{\#}(p)=\mathit{PC}(p)=\mathtt{main}
, and thus using
read-inv
we get

{\langle{v,\mathit{Ver},\ell,{ID}^{\#},{\mathit{PC}}^{\#},\mathit{VS},\mathit{S},{\mathit{A}}^{\#}}\rangle}{}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.8pt}{$\xrightarrow{a}$}}{}{\langle{v,\mathit{Ver},\ell,{ID}^{\#}[p\mapsto{ID}^{\#}(p)\cdot k],{\mathit{PC}}^{\#}[p\mapsto\mathtt{r_{b}}],\mathit{VS},\mathit{S},{\mathit{A}}^{\#}}\rangle}
.

For
Item

8
we have that
ID={ID}^{\#}
and thus
ID[p\mapsto ID(p)\cdot k]={ID}^{\#}[p\mapsto{ID}^{\#}(p)\cdot k]
.

•

read-query
:

Here
a=\mathtt{int}{\langle{p}\rangle}
,
q^{\prime}={\langle{M[m\mapsto\emptyset],ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC}[p\mapsto\mathtt{r_{q}}],\mathit{A}}\rangle}
,
and we have preconditions
\mathit{PC}(p)=\mathtt{r_{b}}
,
k=ID(p)[\mathsf{last}]
, and
m=\mathtt{query}(p,k)
.

All conditions on
q
are maintained under the change to
M
and
\mathit{PC}
.

We get that
{\mathit{PC}}^{\#}(p)=\mathtt{r_{b}}
and since
\ell=0
we can use the transition
read-init
and get

{\langle{v,\mathit{Ver},\ell,{ID}^{\#},{\mathit{PC}}^{\#},\mathit{VS},\mathit{S},{\mathit{A}}^{\#}}\rangle}{}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.8pt}{$\xrightarrow{\mathtt{int}{\langle{p}\rangle}}$}}{}\\
{\langle{v,\mathit{Ver},\ell,{ID}^{\#},{\mathit{PC}}^{\#}[p\mapsto\mathtt{r_{l}}],\mathit{VS}[p\mapsto\{{v}\}],\mathit{S}[p\mapsto\mathit{Ver}],{\mathit{A}}^{\#}}\rangle}
.

For
Item

7
we have that
\mathit{S}[p\mapsto\mathit{Ver}](p)=\mathit{Ver}
.

Given that
\mathit{PC}[p\mapsto\mathtt{r_{q}}](p)=\mathtt{r_{q}}

and
{\mathit{PC}}^{\#}[p\mapsto\mathtt{r_{l}}](p)=\mathtt{r_{l}}
it remains to show:

–

Item

1010c
: we need to show

\mathit{VS}[p\mapsto\{{v}\}](p)=\{{v^{\prime}\;|\;\exists\mathit{ts}^{\prime}\ldotp f(\mathit{S}[p\mapsto\mathit{Ver}](p))\leq{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}\leq f(\mathit{Ver})\land{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}\in H^{\prime}}\}
:

As
H^{\prime}=H
, simplifying both sides we get

\{{v}\}=\{{v^{\prime}\;|\;{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}=f(\mathit{Ver})\in H}\}
which follows from
Item

9
.

–

Item

1010d
:

Indeed, taking
Q\subseteq\{{p^{\prime}\;|\;{\langle{p^{\prime},\_}\rangle}\in M[m\mapsto\emptyset](m)}\}=\{{p^{\prime}\;|\;{\langle{p^{\prime},\_}\rangle}\in\emptyset}\}
we must have
Q=\emptyset
, and it remains to show that for any
Q^{\prime}\subseteq\mathsf{Tid}
with
\left|{Q^{\prime}}\right|>\frac{\left|{\mathsf{Tid}}\right|}{2}
.
we have

\max\{{\mathit{V}(p^{\prime})\;|\;p^{\prime}\in Q^{\prime}}\}\geq f(\mathit{S}(p))
.
Indeed, we get:

\max\{{\mathit{V}(p^{\prime})\;|\;p^{\prime}\in Q^{\prime}}\}\geq\max H=f(\mathit{Ver})\geq f(\mathit{S}(p))

Where we used
Item

2
,
Item

9
,
Item

7
and the monotonicity of
f
.

•

read-update
:

Here
a=\mathtt{int}{\langle{p}\rangle}
,
q^{\prime}={\langle{M[m\mapsto\emptyset],ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC}[p\mapsto\mathtt{r_{u}}],\mathit{A}[p\mapsto v_{\mathit{m}}]}\rangle}
,
and we have preconditions

\mathit{PC}(p)=\mathtt{r_{q}}
,
k=ID(p)[\mathsf{last}]
,

Q\subseteq\{{p\;|\;{\langle{p,\_}\rangle}\in M(\mathtt{query}(p,k))}\}
,
\left|{Q}\right|>\frac{\left|{\mathsf{Tid}}\right|}{2}
,

{\langle{v_{\mathit{m}},\mathit{ts}_{\mathit{m}}}\rangle}=\max\{{{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}\;|\;\exists p^{\prime}\in Q\ldotp{\langle{p^{\prime},{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}}\rangle}\in M(\mathtt{query}(p,k))}\}
,

and
m=\mathtt{update}(p,k,{\langle{v_{\mathit{m}},\mathit{ts}_{\mathit{m}}}\rangle})
.

Note that by definition, and using the fact that
\frac{\left|{\mathsf{Tid}}\right|}{2}>0
we get
H^{\prime}=H(M[m\mapsto\emptyset])=H(M)=H
, and so
Item

2
still holds.

Using
Item

33a
and the above preconditions, we get
Item

33b
and specifically

{\langle{v_{\mathit{m}},\mathit{ts}_{\mathit{m}}}\rangle}={\langle{v_{p},\mathit{ts}_{p}}\rangle}
,
noticing that indeed
v_{p}=v_{\mathit{m}}=\mathit{A}[p\mapsto v_{\mathit{m}}](p)
.

For
Item

4
note that from the above there exists some
p^{\prime}
such that
{\langle{p^{\prime},{\langle{v_{\mathit{m}},\mathit{ts}_{\mathit{m}}}\rangle}}\rangle}\in M(\mathtt{query}(p,k))
, and so the set mentioned in
Item

4
is unchanged by the addition of
\mathtt{update}(p,k,{\langle{v_{\mathit{m}},\mathit{ts}_{\mathit{m}}}\rangle})
.
For every element in that set, there exists a thread
p^{\prime}
such that
\mathit{PC}(p^{\prime})=\mathtt{w_{u}}\neq\mathtt{r_{q}}
, and in particular
p^{\prime}\neq p
and so the condition for each element continues to hold.

Now, as
\mathit{PC}(p)=\mathtt{r_{q}}
, using
Item

1010d
taking the above
Q
and
Q^{\prime}=\emptyset
we get

\max\{{{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}\;|\;\exists p^{\prime}\in Q\ldotp{\langle{p^{\prime},{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}}\rangle}\in M(\mathtt{query}(p,k))}\}\geq f(\mathit{S}(p))
.

That is,
{\langle{v_{p},\mathit{ts}_{p}}\rangle}\geq f(\mathit{S}(p))
.

Consider two cases for the post-state
q^{\prime}
:

–

If
{\langle{v_{p},\mathit{ts}_{p}}\rangle}\notin H^{\prime}
:

We perform an empty sequence of transitions.

We have that
\mathit{PC}[p\mapsto\mathtt{r_{u}}](p)=\mathtt{r_{u}}
,
{\langle{v_{p},\mathit{ts}_{p}}\rangle}\notin H^{\prime}
, and from
\mathit{PC}(p)=\mathtt{r_{q}}
we get
{\mathit{PC}}^{\#}(p)=\mathtt{r_{l}}
.
Togather with the fact that
{\langle{v_{p},\mathit{ts}_{p}}\rangle}\geq f(\mathit{S}(p))
we get
Item

1010e
.

The relevant values for
Item

1010c
are unchanged as
H^{\prime}=H
.

–

If
{\langle{v_{p},\mathit{ts}_{p}}\rangle}\in H^{\prime}
:

From the fact that
\mathit{PC}(p)=\mathtt{r_{q}}
we get that
{\mathit{PC}}^{\#}(p)=\mathtt{r_{l}}
and thus from
Item

1010c
we have
\mathit{VS}(p)=\{{v^{\prime}\;|\;\exists\mathit{ts}^{\prime}\ldotp f(\mathit{S}(p))\leq{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}\leq f(\mathit{Ver})\land{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}\in H}\}
.

We claim that
v_{\mathit{m}}\in\mathit{VS}(p)
. As
{\langle{v_{\mathit{m}},\mathit{ts}_{\mathit{m}}}\rangle}={\langle{v_{p},\mathit{ts}_{p}}\rangle}\in H^{\prime}=H
, it is sufficient to show that

f(\mathit{S}(p))\leq{\langle{v_{p},\mathit{ts}_{p}}\rangle}\leq f(\mathit{Ver})
.
Indeed, we have shown that
{\langle{v_{p},\mathit{ts}_{p}}\rangle}\geq f(\mathit{S}(p))
,
and the fact that
{\langle{v_{p},\mathit{ts}_{p}}\rangle}\leq f(\mathit{Ver})
follows from
f(\mathit{Ver})=\max H
.
Overall, we get that
{\mathit{PC}}^{\#}(p)=\mathtt{r_{l}}
and
v_{\mathit{m}}\in\mathit{VS}(p)
,
and using the rule
read-pick
, we can perform a transition

{\langle{v,\mathit{Ver},\ell,{ID}^{\#},{\mathit{PC}}^{\#},\mathit{VS},\mathit{S},{\mathit{A}}^{\#}}\rangle}{}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.8pt}{$\xrightarrow{\mathtt{int}{\langle{p}\rangle}}$}}{}\\
{\langle{v,\mathit{Ver},\ell,{ID}^{\#},{\mathit{PC}}^{\#}[p\mapsto\mathtt{r_{e}}],\mathit{VS},\mathit{S},{\mathit{A}}^{\#}[p\mapsto v_{\mathit{m}}]}\rangle}
.

For
Item

1010f
we have
\mathit{PC}[p\mapsto\mathtt{r_{u}}](p)=\mathtt{r_{u}}
,
{\langle{v_{p},\mathit{ts}_{p}}\rangle}\in H^{\prime}
,
and
{\mathit{PC}}^{\#}[p\mapsto\mathtt{r_{e}}](p)=\mathtt{r_{e}}
.

For
Item

1010b
we have
\mathit{A}[p\mapsto v_{\mathit{m}}](p)={\mathit{A}}^{\#}[p\mapsto v_{\mathit{m}}](p)=v_{\mathit{m}}
.

write-update
:

Here
a=\mathtt{int}{\langle{p}\rangle}
,
q^{\prime}={\langle{M[m\mapsto\emptyset],ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC}[p\mapsto\mathtt{w_{u}}],\mathit{A}}\rangle}
,
and we have preconditions

\mathit{PC}(p)=\mathtt{w_{q}}
,
k=ID(p)[\mathsf{last}]
,

Q\subseteq\{{p\;|\;{\langle{p,\_}\rangle}\in M(\mathtt{query}(p,k))}\}
,
\left|{Q}\right|>\frac{\left|{\mathsf{Tid}}\right|}{2}
,

{\langle{t,\_}\rangle}=\max\{{\mathit{ts}^{\prime}\;|\;\exists p^{\prime}\in Q,v^{\prime}\ldotp{\langle{p^{\prime},{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}}\rangle}\in M(\mathtt{query}(p,k))}\}
,

and
m=\mathtt{update}(p,k,{\langle{\mathit{A}(p),{\langle{t+1,p}\rangle}}\rangle})
.

Note that by definition, and using the fact that
\frac{\left|{\mathsf{Tid}}\right|}{2}>0
we get
H^{\prime}=H(M[m\mapsto\emptyset])=H(M)=H
, and so
Item

2
still holds.

Using
Item

33a
we get that after the update
M[m\mapsto\emptyset]

Item

33b
holds and specifically
{\langle{v_{p},\mathit{ts}_{p}}\rangle}={\langle{\mathit{A}(p),{\langle{t+1,p}\rangle}}\rangle}
in the post-state
q^{\prime}
.

For
Item

4
the pair
{\langle{\mathit{A}(p),{\langle{t+1,p}\rangle}}\rangle}
is possibly added to the set mentioned in the condition, and satisfies the condition since
\mathit{PC}[p\mapsto\mathtt{w_{u}}](p)=\mathtt{w_{u}}
and
{\langle{v_{p},\mathit{ts}_{p}}\rangle}={\langle{\mathit{A}(p),{\langle{t+1,p}\rangle}}\rangle}
.

From the preconditions we get that there exists some
v^{\prime\prime},p^{\prime\prime}
such that

{\langle{v^{\prime\prime},{\langle{t,p^{\prime\prime}}\rangle}}\rangle}=\max\{{{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}\;|\;\exists p^{\prime}\in Q\ldotp{\langle{p^{\prime},{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}}\rangle}\in M(\mathtt{query}(p,k))}\}

As
\mathit{PC}(p)=\mathtt{w_{q}}
, using
Item

1010d
taking
Q
and
Q^{\prime}=\emptyset
we get

{\langle{v^{\prime\prime},{\langle{t,p^{\prime\prime}}\rangle}}\rangle}=\max\{{{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}\;|\;\exists p^{\prime}\in Q\ldotp{\langle{p^{\prime},{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}}\rangle}\in M(\mathtt{query}(p,k))}\}\geq f(\mathit{S}(p))
.
Overall,
{\langle{v_{p},\mathit{ts}_{p}}\rangle}={\langle{\mathit{A}(p),{\langle{t+1,p}\rangle}}\rangle}>{\langle{v^{\prime\prime},{\langle{t,p^{\prime\prime}}\rangle}}\rangle}\geq f(\mathit{S}(p))
.

Also note that using
Item

1010a
,
\mathit{PC}(p)=\mathtt{w_{q}}
implies that
{\mathit{PC}}^{\#}(p)=\mathtt{w_{w}}
thus from
Item

1010b

\mathit{A}(p)={\mathit{A}}^{\#}(p)
.
Overall we get
v_{p}={\mathit{A}}^{\#}(p)
.

Now we consider three cases:

–

If
{\langle{v_{p},\mathit{ts}_{p}}\rangle}\notin H
:

We perform an empty sequence of transitions.

Using what we proved above, we get that
Item

1010e
and
Item

1010b
hold.

–

If
{\langle{v_{p},\mathit{ts}_{p}}\rangle}=\max H

Since
{\mathit{PC}}^{\#}({p})=\mathtt{w_{w}}
and
\ell=0
, we can use the rule
write-top
and get a transition

{\langle{v,\mathit{Ver},\ell,{ID}^{\#},{\mathit{PC}}^{\#},\mathit{VS},\mathit{S},{\mathit{A}}^{\#}}\rangle}{}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.8pt}{$\xrightarrow{\mathtt{int}{\langle{p}\rangle}}$}}{}\\
{\langle{{\mathit{A}}^{\#}(p),\mathit{Ver}+1,\ell,{ID}^{\#},{\mathit{PC}}^{\#}[p\mapsto\mathtt{w_{e}}],\mathit{VS},\mathit{S},{\mathit{A}}^{\#}}\rangle}

For
Item

6
and
Item

7
we can use
\mathit{Ver}<\mathit{Ver}+1
.

To show the conditions on
{\langle{q^{\prime},{{q}^{\#}}^{\prime}}\rangle}
hold we define
f^{\prime}:\{{0{,}\ldots{,}\mathit{Ver}+1}\}\to\mathsf{Val}\times\mathsf{TS}
by:

f^{\prime}(t)=\begin{cases}f(t)&t\leq\mathit{Ver}\\
{\langle{v_{p},\mathit{ts}_{p}}\rangle}&t=\mathit{Ver}+1\end{cases}

This function is monotonic since
f^{\prime}(\mathit{Ver}+1)={\langle{v_{p},\mathit{ts}_{p}}\rangle}=\max H=f(\mathit{Ver})=f^{\prime}(\mathit{Ver})
and
f
is monotonic.

For
Item

9
we have
f^{\prime}(\mathit{Ver}+1)=\max H={\langle{{\mathit{A}}^{\#}(p),\_}\rangle}
, where for the last equality we use
v_{p}={\mathit{A}}^{\#}(p)
, proved above.

For conditions on each thread
p^{\prime\prime}\in\mathsf{Tid}
(aside from
Item

1010a
which is easily verified):
Conditions dependent only on
f^{\prime}(\mathit{S}(p^{\prime\prime}))
hold as they did before the transition since using
Item

7
we have
\mathit{S}(p^{\prime\prime})\leq\mathit{Ver}<\mathit{Ver}+1
and thus
f^{\prime}(\mathit{S}(p^{\prime\prime}))=f(\mathit{S}(p^{\prime\prime}))
.
For
Item

1010c
we additionally use
f^{\prime}(\mathit{Ver}+1)=f(\mathit{Ver})
.

Item

1010f
clearly holds for
p^{\prime\prime}=p
.
All other values relevant for conditions are unchanged.

–

If
{\langle{v_{p},\mathit{ts}_{p}}\rangle}\in H
and
{\langle{v_{p},\mathit{ts}_{p}}\rangle}<\max H

As we have shown above
f(\mathit{S}(p))\leq{\langle{v_{p},\mathit{ts}_{p}}\rangle}
, and using
\max H=f(\mathit{Ver})
we get
f(\mathit{S}(p))<f(\mathit{Ver})
and thus from monotonicity of
f
(in the contra-positive) we get
\mathit{S}(p)<\mathit{Ver}
.

Together with the fact that
{\mathit{PC}}^{\#}({p})=\mathtt{w_{w}}
and
\ell=0
, we can use the rule
write-roll-back
and get a transition

{\langle{v,\mathit{Ver},\ell,{ID}^{\#},{\mathit{PC}}^{\#},\mathit{VS},\mathit{S},{\mathit{A}}^{\#}}\rangle}{}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.8pt}{$\xrightarrow{\mathtt{int}{\langle{p}\rangle}}$}}{}\\
{\langle{{\mathit{A}}^{\#}(p),\mathit{Ver}-1,1,{ID}^{\#},{\mathit{PC}}^{\#}[p\mapsto\mathtt{w_{r}}],\mathit{VS},\mathit{S},{\mathit{A}}^{\#}[p\mapsto v]}\rangle}

Then, Since
{\mathit{PC}}^{\#}[p\mapsto\mathtt{w_{r}}](p)=\mathtt{w_{r}}
we can perform a transition using the rule
write-roll-forward
and get a transition

{\langle{{\mathit{A}}^{\#}(p),\mathit{Ver}-1,1,{ID}^{\#},{\mathit{PC}}^{\#}[p\mapsto\mathtt{w_{r}}],\mathit{VS},\mathit{S},{\mathit{A}}^{\#}[p\mapsto v]}\rangle}\\
{}\mathrel{\raisebox{-0.8pt}{$\xrightarrow{\mathtt{int}{\langle{p}\rangle}}$}}{}\\
{\langle{{\mathit{A}}^{\#}[p\mapsto v](p),\mathit{Ver}-1+1,0,{ID}^{\#},{\mathit{PC}}^{\#}[p\mapsto\mathtt{w_{e}}],\mathit{VS},\mathit{S},{\mathit{A}}^{\#}[p\mapsto v]}\rangle}
.

Simplifying this we get a state

{\langle{v,\mathit{Ver},0,{ID}^{\#},{\mathit{PC}}^{\#}[p\mapsto\mathtt{w_{e}}],\mathit{VS},\mathit{S},{\mathit{A}}^{\#}[p\mapsto v]}\rangle}

For
Item

5
note that
\ell=0
in the post-state
{{q}^{\#}}^{\prime}
.

Additionally,
Item

1010a
and
Item

1010f
clearly hold for
p
.

server-query-ack
:

Here
a=\mathtt{int}{\langle{p}\rangle}
and
q^{\prime}={\langle{M^{\prime},ID,\mathit{V},\mathit{PC},\mathit{A}}\rangle}
where

m=\mathtt{query}(\_,\_)\in\textsf{dom}{({M})}
,

\forall{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}.{\langle{p,{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}}\rangle}\notin M(m)
, and

M^{\prime}=M[m\mapsto M(m)\uplus\{{{\langle{p,\mathit{V}(p)}\rangle}}\}]
.

For
Item

4
we note that the set mentioned in the condition is unchanged as
\mathit{V}(p)
is already in it, and the condition for each element in the set still holds as
\mathit{PC}
is unchanged.

We perform an empty sequence of transitions

Let
p^{\prime\prime},k^{\prime\prime}
be the identifiers such that
m=\mathtt{query}(p^{\prime\prime},k^{\prime\prime})
.
All conditions on
{\langle{q^{\prime},{{q}^{\#}}^{\prime}}\rangle}
for all threads except
p^{\prime\prime}
are unaffected.
We show that if
k^{\prime\prime}=ID(p^{\prime\prime})[\mathsf{last}]
and
\mathit{PC}(p^{\prime\prime})\in\{{\mathtt{r_{q}},\mathtt{w_{q}}}\}
, then
Item

1010d
still holds for
p^{\prime\prime}
.

Indeed, Let
Q\subseteq\{{p^{\prime}\;|\;{\langle{p^{\prime},\_}\rangle}\in M^{\prime}(m)}\}
and
Q^{\prime}\subseteq\mathsf{Tid}\setminus Q
be sets such that
\left|{Q\uplus Q^{\prime}}\right|>\frac{\left|{\mathsf{Tid}}\right|}{2}
.
If
p\notin Q
we have
Q\subseteq\{{p^{\prime}\;|\;{\langle{p^{\prime},\_}\rangle}\in M(m)}\}
and from
Item

1010d
holding in the pre-states
{\langle{q,{q}^{\#}}\rangle}
we get

\max\{{{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}\;|\;\exists p^{\prime}\in Q\ldotp{\langle{p^{\prime},{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}}\rangle}\in M(m)}\}\cup\{{\mathit{V}(p^{\prime})\;|\;p^{\prime}\in Q^{\prime}}\}\geq f(\mathit{S}(p))
.
As
M^{\prime}(m)={\langle{p,\mathit{V}(p)}\rangle}\uplus M(m)
and
p\notin Q
we get

\{{{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}\;|\;\exists p^{\prime}\in Q\ldotp{\langle{p^{\prime},{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}}\rangle}\in M(m)}\}=\{{{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}\;|\;\exists p^{\prime}\in Q\ldotp{\langle{p^{\prime},{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}}\rangle}\in M^{\prime}(m)}\}

and overall
\max\{{{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}\;|\;\exists p^{\prime}\in Q\ldotp{\langle{p^{\prime},{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}}\rangle}\in M^{\prime}(m)}\}\cup\{{\mathit{V}(p^{\prime})\;|\;p^{\prime}\in Q^{\prime}}\}\geq f(\mathit{S}(p))
.

Otherwise, consider
\hat{Q}=Q\setminus\{{p}\},\hat{Q^{\prime}}=Q^{\prime}\cup\{{p}\}
.
As
M^{\prime}=M[m\mapsto M(m)\uplus\{{{\langle{p,\mathit{V}(p)}\rangle}}\}]
,
we get that
\hat{Q}\subseteq\{{p^{\prime}\;|\;{\langle{p^{\prime},\_}\rangle}\in M(m)}\}
.
Additionally,
\hat{Q^{\prime}}\subseteq\mathsf{Tid}\setminus\hat{Q}
and
\left|{\hat{Q}\uplus\hat{Q^{\prime}}}\right|=\left|{Q\uplus Q^{\prime}}\right|>\frac{\left|{\mathsf{Tid}}\right|}{2}
.
Thus from the assumption that
Item

1010d
holds in the pre-states we get

\max\{{{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}\;|\;\exists p^{\prime}\in\hat{Q}\ldotp{\langle{p^{\prime},{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}}\rangle}\in M(m)}\}\cup\{{\mathit{V}(p^{\prime})\;|\;p^{\prime}\in\hat{Q^{\prime}}}\}\geq f(\mathit{S}(p))
.
Using the fact that
M^{\prime}(m)={\langle{p,\mathit{V}(p)}\rangle}\uplus M(m)
,
p\notin\hat{Q}
, and
p\in\hat{Q^{\prime}}
we have:

\displaystyle\{{{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}\;|\;\exists p^{\prime}\in\hat{Q}\ldotp{\langle{p^{\prime},{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}}\rangle}\in M(m)}\}

\displaystyle\cup\{{\mathit{V}(p^{\prime})\;|\;p^{\prime}\in\hat{Q^{\prime}}}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{{{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}\;|\;\exists p^{\prime}\in\hat{Q}\ldotp{\langle{p^{\prime},{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}}\rangle}\in M^{\prime}(m)}\}\cup\{{\mathit{V}(p)}\}

\displaystyle\cup\{{\mathit{V}(p^{\prime})\;|\;p^{\prime}\in Q^{\prime}}\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{{{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}\;|\;\exists p^{\prime}\in Q\ldotp{\langle{p^{\prime},{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}}\rangle}\in M^{\prime}(m)}\}

\displaystyle\cup\{{\mathit{V}(p^{\prime})\;|\;p^{\prime}\in Q^{\prime}}\}

And we get again the desired inequality:

\max\{{{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}\;|\;\exists p^{\prime}\in Q\ldotp{\langle{p^{\prime},{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}}\rangle}\in M^{\prime}(m)}\}\cup\{{\mathit{V}(p^{\prime})\;|\;p^{\prime}\in Q^{\prime}}\}\geq f(\mathit{S}(p))
.

server-update-ack
:

Here
a=\mathtt{int}{\langle{p}\rangle}
and
q^{\prime}={\langle{M^{\prime},ID,\mathit{V}^{\prime},\mathit{PC},\mathit{A}}\rangle}
where

m=\mathtt{update}(\_,\_,{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle})\in\textsf{dom}{({M})}
,
M^{\prime}=M[m\mapsto M(m)\uplus\{{p}\}]
, and
\mathit{V}^{\prime}=\mathit{V}[p\mapsto\max\{{\mathit{V}(p),{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}}\}]
.

For
Item

1
we have that
p\in M^{\prime}(m)
and as required
\mathit{V}^{\prime}(p)\geq{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}
, and other needed inequalities are preserved when increasing
\mathit{V}^{\prime}(p)
.

For
Item

4
we note that the set mentioned in the condition is unchanged as both
\mathit{V}(p)
and
{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}
are already in it, and the condition for each element in the set still holds as
\mathit{PC}
is unchanged.

Item

2
will be justified based on the cases considered below.

Specifically, by definition of
H
we either have
H^{\prime}=H
or
H^{\prime}=H\uplus\{{{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}}\}
. We consider both cases:

–

If
H^{\prime}=H
:

For
Item

2
,
\mathit{V}^{\prime}(p)\geq\mathit{V}(p)
and so given any
Q
,
\max\{{\mathit{V}^{\prime}(p^{\prime})\;|\;p^{\prime}\in Q}\}
can only increase, while
\max H^{\prime}=\max H
is unchanged.

We perform an empty sequence of transitions

Note that the change to
M^{\prime}(m)
where
m
is an update message can only possibly effect
H^{\prime}
, but here
H^{\prime}=H
.

For
Item

1010d
we similarly have that a maximum involving
\mathit{V}(p)
is an upper bound on another value and this still holds when replacing it with
\mathit{V}^{\prime}(p)
.

–

If
H^{\prime}=H\uplus\{{{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}}\}
:

For
Item

2
, let
Q\subseteq\mathsf{Tid}
be a set such that
\left|{Q}\right|>\frac{\left|{\mathsf{Tid}}\right|}{2}
.
We have that
{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}\in H^{\prime}
and thus
\left|{M^{\prime}(m)}\right|>\frac{\left|{\mathsf{Tid}}\right|}{2}
.
Therefore, there must exist some
\tilde{p}\in Q\cap M^{\prime}(m)
.
We get that
\max\{{\mathit{V}^{\prime}(p^{\prime})\;|\;p^{\prime}\in Q}\}\geq\mathit{V}^{\prime}(\tilde{p})\geq{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}
, where the last inequality uses

1

Furthermore, similarly to the previous case as
\mathit{V}
only increases point-wise we have using

.
Combining these we get

\max\{{\mathit{V}^{\prime}(p^{\prime})\;|\;p^{\prime}\in Q}\}\geq\max\{{{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle},\max H}\}=\max H^{\prime}
.

Now, since
{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}\notin H
and

, using

, taking

,
\mathit{PC}(p^{\prime})=\mathtt{w_{u}}
,
and
{\langle{v^{\prime},\mathit{ts}^{\prime}}\rangle}={\langle{v_{p^{\prime}},\mathit{ts}_{p^{\prime}}}\rangle}\notin H
.
Thus using

1010e

f(\mathit{S}({p^{\prime}}))\leq{\langle{v_{p^{\prime}},\mathit{ts}_{p^{\prime}}}\rangle}
,
{\mathit{PC}}^{\#}({p^{\prime}})=\mathtt{w_{w}}
, and thus from

\mathit{A}({p^{\prime}})={\mathit{A}}^{\#}({p^{\prime}})
.
Using

we have that
v_{p^{\prime}}=\mathit{A}({p^{\prime}})
, and overall this gives
v_{p^{\prime}}={\mathit{A}}^{\#}({p^{\prime}})
.

We consider two sub-cases:

*

If
{\langle{v_{p^{\prime}},\mathit{ts}_{p^{\prime}}}\rangle}=\max H^{\prime}

Since

{\langle{{\mathit{A}}^{\#}(p^{\prime}),\mathit{Ver}+1,\ell,{ID}^{\#},{\mathit{PC}}^{\#}[p^{\prime}\mapsto\mathtt{w_{e}}],\mathit{VS},\mathit{S},{\mathit{A}}^{\#}}\rangle}

For

To show the conditions on
{\langle{q^{\prime},{{q}^{\#}}^{\prime}}\rangle}
hold we define

{\langle{v_{p^{\prime}},\mathit{ts}_{p^{\prime}}}\rangle}&t=\mathit{Ver}+1\end{cases}
